<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> Every continuous, odd, absolutely irreducible two-dimensional 2-adic representation of $`G_{\mathbb Q}`$ that is unramified outside finitely many finite primes occurs in the full completed Hecke algebra at some odd tame level. The level may contain auxiliary tame primes, and scalar and reducible residual representations are included. This is a completed-Hecke occurrence result, with no de Rham hypothesis; it does not assert classical modularity.

- **claim-unrestricted-dyadic-pro-modularity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Let $E/\mathbb Q_2$ be finite, with ring of integers $\mathcal O_E$, and let $r:G_{\mathbb Q}\longrightarrow\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}_2(E)$ be continuous and absolutely irreducible. Suppose that $r$ is unramified outside finitely many finite primes and is odd: $\det r(c)=-1$ for complex conjugation $c$. Then there exist an odd positive integer $N$, divisible by every odd prime where $r$ ramifies, and a continuous $\mathbb Z_2$-algebra homomorphism $$\lambda:\mathbb T_2(N)\longrightarrow\mathcal O_E$$ such that, for every prime $\ell\nmid2N$, $$\lambda(T_\ell)=\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}r(\operatorname{Frob}_\ell),\qquad \lambda(\ell S_\ell)=\det r(\operatorname{Frob}_\ell).$$
  - Explanation: An odd, continuous, absolutely irreducible dyadic representation with finite ramification occurs in the full completed Hecke algebra at a suitable odd tame level. The conclusion identifies both Frobenius traces and determinants and imposes no geometric local condition on the original representation.
- **claim-cyclotomic-determinant-normalization** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-normalization)): After a finite coefficient extension and a twist through $\varepsilon$, one may arrange $$\det r=\chi=\delta\varepsilon^w,$$ where $\delta$ has finite order and is unramified at $2$. The integer $w$ may be chosen arbitrarily large in one parity class. It may in particular be chosen so that $\chi$ is the determinant of a regular cuspidal modular representation that is not virtually solvable. Oddness is preserved. The character $\psi=\chi\varepsilon$, viewed on $\mathbb Z_2^\times$ by local reciprocity, is $z\mapsto z^{w+1}$.
  - Explanation: A finite coefficient extension and continuous cyclotomic twist normalize the determinant without changing oddness. The determinant can then be supplied by a regular cuspidal modular representation independently of the original residual representation.
- **claim-large-horizontal-trace-family** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-large-family)): Fix a finite ramification set $S$ containing $2$, infinity, and the ramified primes of the normalized $r$. There is a reduced horizontal framed deformation component $B$ of determinant $\chi$ through $r$. Its completed trace image $D$ is a complete local $\mathcal O$-domain, contains the coefficient point of $r$, and satisfies $$\dim B\ge6,\qquad \dim D\ge3.$$ Moreover, $B$ has a coefficient point whose representation is not virtually solvable. Consequently the restriction of this family to any finite extension of $\mathbb Q$ is generically absolutely irreducible.
  - Explanation: The normalized representation lies on a large horizontal framed component whose trace domain retains its coefficient point. Avoiding the virtually solvable trace loci supplies a coefficient point that remains absolutely irreducible after finite restriction.
- **claim-potential-support-with-independent-residue** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-potential-support)): Let $B$ be the family in Lemma <a href="#lem:large-family" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:large-family">3</a>, with the normalized determinant of Lemma <a href="#lem:normalization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:normalization">2</a>. There is a solvable totally real Galois extension $F/\mathbb Q$, of even degree and completely split at $2$, such that the closed trace image of $B\|_{G_F}$ is pro-modular at one fixed tame level and central character $\psi\|_{G_F}$. This assertion includes scalar and reducible semisimple residual representations.
  - Explanation: After solvable totally real base change split at the dyadic prime, the entire restricted trace family has completed Hecke support at a common tame level and central character. Scalar and reducible semisimple residue are included.
- **claim-finite-diagonal-family-module** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-family)): For the normalized representation $r$, the preceding constructions give a horizontal complete local domain $D$ of dimension at least three containing its coefficient point, and continuous maps $$\mathbb T\longrightarrow D,\qquad R\longrightarrow D$$ that make $D$ finite over both source rings. There is a right $\mathcal E^{\widehat\otimes b}$-module $L_0$, finite and faithful over $\mathbb T$, whose inverse under the product block equivalence is the residual summand of compact definite forms. The module $$L=\bigl(L_0\otimes_{\mathbb T}D\bigr) \big/\bigl(\text{$D$-torsion}\bigr)$$ is nonzero, finite and torsion-free over $D$. All $b$ central $R$-actions on $L$ are the same map $R\to D$. Its inverse under the product block equivalence is finite over the completed group algebra of a sufficiently small product of determinant-one compact subgroups.
  - Explanation: The trace domain is finite over both the base-changed Hecke algebra and the single local pseudodeformation ring. A nonzero torsion-free coefficient module carries equal central local actions and reconstructs an admissible product object.
- **claim-one-factor-admissibility** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-diagonal)): Let $D$ be a complete noetherian local $\mathcal O$-domain in which $\varpi\ne0$, and suppose that $R\to D$ is a finite continuous local map. Let $L\ne0$ be a finite torsion-free $D$-module with a commuting continuous right $\mathcal E^{(b)}$-action. Suppose that every copy of $R$ acts through the same map $R\to D$, and that $$M=L\widehat\otimes_{\mathcal E^{(b)}}P^{(b)}$$ is admissible in the compact category. Then, using the first $\mathcal E$-action, $$V=L\widehat\otimes_{\mathcal E}P$$ is nonzero, $\mathcal O$-torsion-free, and finite over $\mathscr A=\mathcal O[[K]]$, for every sufficiently small uniform open subgroup $K\subset\mathop{\mathrm{SL}}_2(\mathbb Z_2)$. It has a continuous commuting $D$-action.
  - Explanation: Equal local parameters allow product admissibility to imply admissibility of the object reconstructed from a single algebra action. Nonvanishing, coefficient torsion-freeness and the commuting trace-domain action are retained.
- **claim-dyadic-local-block-input** (classification, [source](#stmt-input-local-blocks)): The following statements hold for every such block at $p=2$, including the block of a scalar residual Galois representation.* 1. *The functor $\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}(P,-)$ is an exact equivalence from the compact block to right pseudocompact $\mathcal E$-modules, with inverse $-\widehat\otimes_{\mathcal E}P$. The algebra $\mathcal E$ is finite over $R$.* 2. *Let $\mathrm{CH}_R$ be the universal Cayley–Hamilton algebra for the local pseudorepresentation, and let $\mathrm{CH}_R^{\mathrm{tf}}$ be its maximal $\mathcal O$-torsion-free quotient. There is an $R$-compatible identification $$\mathrm{CH}_R^{\mathrm{tf}}\simeq \begin{cases} (e\mathcal E e)^{\mathrm{op}},&\text{in a nonsupersingular block},\\ M_2((e\mathcal E e)^{\mathrm{op}}),&\text{in a supersingular block}. \end{cases}$$ The corner equivalence is induced by the Montréal functor on the quotient by the objects with trivial $\mathop{\mathrm{SL}}_2(\mathbb Q_2)$-action.* 3. *An $\mathcal E$-module finite and torsion-free over $\mathcal O$ gives, after duality and inversion of $2$, an admissible Banach representation of finite length. At an absolutely irreducible Galois coefficient parameter there is a unique absolutely irreducible Banach object. At a sum of characters $\gamma_1+\gamma_2$, the simple objects have the following description. If the characters coincide, the unique simple is $\mathop{\mathrm{Ind}}_B^G(1\otimes\varepsilon^{-1})^{\mathrm{cts}}\otimes\gamma_1\circ\det$. If their sum is $\gamma+\gamma\varepsilon$, the simples are $$\{1,\widehat{\mathrm{St}}, \mathop{\mathrm{Ind}}_B^G(\varepsilon\otimes\varepsilon^{-1})^{\mathrm{cts}}\} \otimes\gamma\circ\det,$$ where $\widehat{\mathrm{St}}$ is continuous Steinberg. If $\gamma_1\gamma_2^{-1}\notin\{1,\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{-1}\}$, they are exactly $$\mathop{\mathrm{Ind}}_B^G(\gamma_1\otimes\gamma_2\varepsilon^{-1})^{\mathrm{cts}}, \qquad \mathop{\mathrm{Ind}}_B^G(\gamma_2\otimes\gamma_1\varepsilon^{-1})^{\mathrm{cts}}.$$ Here $B$ is the upper Borel, induction is unnormalized, and Galois characters are viewed as characters of $\mathbb Q_2^\times$ by reciprocity.
  - Explanation: The compact block equivalence, finite endomorphism algebra, integral Cayley–Hamilton corner comparison and coefficient-fiber Banach classification apply at the dyadic prime, including scalar residual blocks.
- **claim-finite-coinvariants-and-base-change** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-coinvariant-finite)): The module $C_K$ is finite over $R$. For a finite pseudocompact right $\mathcal E$-module $W$, $$(W\widehat\otimes_{\mathcal E}P)/(\varpi,K-1) =W\otimes_{\mathcal E}C_K.$$ The analogous formula holds for product coinvariants. If a commutative complete coefficient algebra $A$, finite over $R$ (over $R^{\widehat\otimes b}$ in the product case), acts on $W$ compatibly and commutes with its algebra action, these formulas commute with base change $A\to H$ to a field.
  - Explanation: Coinvariants of the projective generator are finite over the local coefficient ring. Reconstruction converts coinvariants into ordinary tensor products, including product objects and the stated field base changes.
- **claim-full-noncharacter-corner-on-curves** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-full-corner)): At the generic point of a nonconstant characteristic-two curve, the idempotent $e$ is full: if $H$ is a finite extension of the curve’s fraction field and $\mathcal E_H=\mathcal E\otimes_RH$, then $\mathcal E_He\mathcal E_H=\mathcal E_H$.
  - Explanation: The quotient supported on objects with trivial determinant-one action disappears away from the closed residual point. Consequently the noncharacter corner becomes full at the generic point of a nonconstant special-fiber curve.
- **claim-principal-series-simultaneous-survival** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-principal-families)): Suppose a nonconstant characteristic-two curve has generic local parameter $\gamma_1+\gamma_2$, with $\gamma_1\ne\gamma_2$. After a finite extension, let $J=k'[[t]]$ be its normalization, finite over the curve ring, and $H=\mathop{\mathrm{Frac}}(J)$. The compact dual principal-series families over $J$ with inducing orders $$(\gamma_1,\gamma_2\varepsilon^{-1}),\qquad (\gamma_2,\gamma_1\varepsilon^{-1})$$ correspond to finite free $J$-modules $W_1,W_2$ for $\mathcal E$. Their central $R$-action is the given curve parameter. Their generic fibers are the two distinct simple right $\mathcal E_H$-modules, and $$(W_1\otimes_JH)\otimes_{\mathcal E_H}(C_K\otimes_RH)\ne0 \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad (W_2\otimes_JH)\otimes_{\mathcal E_H}(C_K\otimes_RH)\ne0.$$
  - Explanation: The opposite inducing orders extend to finite free integral families whose generic fibers are distinct simple modules. Their determinant-one stabilizer characters are inverse, which forces simultaneous coinvariant vanishing.
- **claim-uniform-survival-on-curves** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-curve-support)): Let $H$ be a finite extension of the fraction field of a nonconstant characteristic-two curve in $\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}R$, large enough to split the parameter and the finite algebra $\mathcal E_H$. If $C_{K,H}=C_K\otimes_RH\ne0$, then $$S\otimes_{\mathcal E_H}C_{K,H}\ne0$$ for every simple right $\mathcal E_H$-module $S$.
  - Explanation: A nonzero generic coinvariant fiber survives tensoring with every simple module of the split algebra. This uniform survival is the obstruction to a nonconstant curve appearing in single-factor coinvariants of an admissible product.
- **claim-growth-drop-and-positive-rank** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-growth-drop)): Let $M$ be a finite $\mathscr A$-module and let $f:M\to M$ be injective and $\mathscr A$-linear. If $M/fM\ne0$, then $$g(M/fM)\leq g(M)-1.$$ Moreover, a finite $\mathscr A$-module of growth degree four has positive rank over $\mathscr A$.
  - Explanation: An injective endomorphism with nonzero cokernel decreases Hilbert growth degree. Maximal growth excludes torsion over the Iwasawa algebra and therefore implies positive rank.
- **claim-positive-iwasawa-rank** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-positive-rank)): The module $V$ satisfies $$\mathop{\mathrm{rank}}_{\mathscr A}V>0.$$
  - Explanation: Successive prime specializations compare the dimension of the trace domain with growth of the reconstructed module. An infinite-dimensional coefficient fiber forces maximal growth and hence positive Iwasawa rank.
- **claim-capture-cuspidal-algebraic-type** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-type-capture)): There is an irreducible algebraic representation $U=\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}^n(E^2)\otimes\det^a$, with $n\geq0$, $a\in\mathbb Z$, and $n+2a=d$, such that $$\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_{K_0}(\sigma\otimes U,\Pi_V)\ne0.$$ This Hom space is finite-dimensional and carries a continuous commuting action of $D$.
  - Explanation: Positive Iwasawa rank and polynomial approximation detect an algebraic representation with the prescribed central degree and smooth cuspidal type. Its finite-dimensional multiplicity space carries the trace-domain action.
- **claim-type-forces-local-irreducibility** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-type-irreducible)): Let $E'/E$ be a finite coefficient extension, let $\Pi$ be one of the $E'$-Banach simples described in Theorem <a href="#input:local-blocks" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="input:local-blocks">7</a>, and let $U=\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}^n(E'^2)\otimes\det^a$, with $n\geq0$ and $a\in\mathbb Z$. If $$\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_{K_0}(\sigma\otimes U,\Pi)\ne0,$$ then the Galois parameter of $\Pi$ is absolutely irreducible.
  - Explanation: The inflated sign type tensored with an algebraic representation cannot occur in any reducible-parameter Banach simple in the stated classification. Its occurrence therefore forces an absolutely irreducible local parameter.
- **claim-auxiliary-regular-classical-point** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-regular-point)): There is a coefficient point $x:D\to\mathcal O'$ at which the global pseudorepresentation is that of a continuous absolutely irreducible representation $$r_x:G_{\mathbb Q}\longrightarrow\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}_2(E'), \qquad E'=\mathop{\mathrm{Frac}}(\mathcal O'),$$ after a finite coefficient extension if necessary. This representation is odd and finitely ramified. Its restriction to $G_{\mathbb Q_2}$ is absolutely irreducible and regular de Rham, and $\mathop{\mathrm{WD}}(r_x\|_{G_{\mathbb Q_2}})$ is trivial on wild inertia. Consequently $r_x$ is attached to a classical cuspidal eigenform up to Tate twist.
  - Explanation: A coefficient eigensystem in the captured type produces an auxiliary point with an absolutely irreducible regular de Rham local representation and tame Weil–Deligne parameter. The regular modularity theorem makes this auxiliary representation classical up to Tate twist.
- **claim-dyadic-level-evaluates-at-odd-tame-level** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-hecke-level)): Let $N\geq5$ be odd, and let $f$ be a classical cuspidal eigenform of positive weight and level dividing $2^aN$, for some $a\geq0$. Its eigenvalues away from $2N$ define a continuous homomorphism $$A(N)\longrightarrow\mathcal O_{E_f}$$ after choosing a finite $2$-adic coefficient field $E_f$ containing them. The associated determinant has the Frobenius polynomials of $f$ in <a href="#eq:hecke-frobenius" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:hecke-frobenius">[eq:hecke-frobenius]</a>.
  - Explanation: A classical cuspidal packet with additional dyadic level gives a continuous coefficient point of the full Hecke algebra at the stated odd tame level. Evaluation on the ordinary tower supplies continuity for the specified finite-weight topology.
- **claim-continuous-cyclotomic-twist-stability** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-hecke-twists)): Let $N\geq5$ be odd. Let $\lambda:A(N)\to\mathcal O_E$ be a continuous coefficient point, with associated two-dimensional determinant $(t_\lambda,d_\lambda)$. If $E'/E$ is finite and $\theta:\mathbb Z_2^\times\to\mathcal O_{E'}^\times$ is continuous, then the twisted determinant $$\bigl((\theta\circ\varepsilon)t_\lambda, (\theta\circ\varepsilon)^2d_\lambda\bigr)$$ is also supplied by a continuous coefficient point of $A(N)$. In particular this holds for every integer Tate twist, with the same tame level $N$.
  - Explanation: Continuous characters of the dyadic units twist both the trace and determinant of a Hecke coefficient point while preserving the tame level. This permits both determinant normalization and its reversal.
- **claim-full-adjoint-tangent-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-tangent-bound)): Let $r_x:G_{\mathbb Q,S}\to\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}_2(E_x)$ be odd, absolutely irreducible, and classical cuspidal up to Tate twist. Suppose its restriction to $G_{\mathbb Q_2}$ is absolutely irreducible and de Rham with distinct Hodge–Tate weights, and its Weil–Deligne representation is trivial on wild inertia. Here $S$ is any finite set containing $2$ and all ramified primes. Then $$\dim_{E_x}H^1(G_{\mathbb Q,S},\mathop{\mathrm{ad}}r_x)\leq3.$$ Consequently, if $R_{\mathrm{glob}}$ is the universal global determinant deformation ring with varying determinant for the semisimplified residual data of $r_x$, and $\mathfrak x$ is its coefficient prime, then $$\mathop{\mathrm{edim}}(R_{\mathrm{glob}})_{\mathfrak x}\leq3.$$ Both assertions permit scalar or reducible semisimplified residual data and finite extension of the coefficient ring.
  - Explanation: Adjoint Selmer vanishing and the local cohomology comparison bound the full varying-determinant tangent space at the auxiliary classical point. The argument permits scalar and reducible semisimplified residual data.
- **claim-regular-point-forces-support-factorization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-regular-support)): Let $R_0$ be a complete Noetherian local $\mathcal O$-algebra, and let $A_0$ and $D_0$ be quotients of $R_0$, with $D_0$ a domain. Suppose every irreducible component of $\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}A_0$ has dimension four. Let $x_0:R_0\to\mathcal O'$ be a continuous coefficient point factoring through both quotients, where $\mathcal O'/\mathcal O$ is a finite extension of integer coefficient rings. If $$\mathop{\mathrm{edim}}(R_0)_{\mathfrak x_0}\leq3, \qquad \mathfrak x_0=\ker x_0,$$ then $R_0\to D_0$ factors through $A_0$.
  - Explanation: At a shared coefficient point, the component dimension and embedding-dimension bound force the ambient localized ring to be regular and the Hecke kernel to vanish. The domain hypothesis then spreads that vanishing to the entire trace quotient.

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-input-local-blocks",
        "exact_quote": "The following statements hold for every such block at $p=2$, including the block of a scalar residual Galois representation.*\n\n1.  *The functor $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Hom}}(P,-)$ is an exact equivalence from the compact block to right pseudocompact $\\mathcal E$-modules, with inverse $-\\widehat\\otimes_{\\mathcal E}P$. The algebra $\\mathcal E$ is finite over $R$.*\n\n2.  *Let $\\mathrm{CH}_R$ be the universal Cayley–Hamilton algebra for the local pseudorepresentation, and let $\\mathrm{CH}_R^{\\mathrm{tf}}$ be its maximal $\\mathcal O$-torsion-free quotient. There is an $R$-compatible identification $$\\mathrm{CH}_R^{\\mathrm{tf}}\\simeq\n     \\begin{cases}\n      (e\\mathcal E e)^{\\mathrm{op}},&\\text{in a nonsupersingular block},\\\\\n      M_2((e\\mathcal E e)^{\\mathrm{op}}),&\\text{in a supersingular block}.\n     \\end{cases}$$ The corner equivalence is induced by the Montréal functor on the quotient by the objects with trivial $\\mathop{\\mathrm{SL}}_2(\\mathbb Q_2)$-action.*\n\n3.  *An $\\mathcal E$-module finite and torsion-free over $\\mathcal O$ gives, after duality and inversion of $2$, an admissible Banach representation of finite length. At an absolutely irreducible Galois coefficient parameter there is a unique absolutely irreducible Banach object. At a sum of characters $\\gamma_1+\\gamma_2$, the simple objects have the following description. If the characters coincide, the unique simple is $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Ind}}_B^G(1\\otimes\\varepsilon^{-1})^{\\mathrm{cts}}\\otimes\\gamma_1\\circ\\det$. If their sum is $\\gamma+\\gamma\\varepsilon$, the simples are $$\\{1,\\widehat{\\mathrm{St}},\n           \\mathop{\\mathrm{Ind}}_B^G(\\varepsilon\\otimes\\varepsilon^{-1})^{\\mathrm{cts}}\\}\n           \\otimes\\gamma\\circ\\det,$$ where $\\widehat{\\mathrm{St}}$ is continuous Steinberg. If $\\gamma_1\\gamma_2^{-1}\\notin\\{1,\\varepsilon,\\varepsilon^{-1}\\}$, they are exactly $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{Ind}}_B^G(\\gamma_1\\otimes\\gamma_2\\varepsilon^{-1})^{\\mathrm{cts}},\n     \\qquad\n     \\mathop{\\mathrm{Ind}}_B^G(\\gamma_2\\otimes\\gamma_1\\varepsilon^{-1})^{\\mathrm{cts}}.$$ Here $B$ is the upper Borel, induction is unnormalized, and Galois characters are viewed as characters of $\\mathbb Q_2^\\times$ by reciprocity.",
        "source_id": "PM-010.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-input-local-blocks",
          "exact_quote": "The following statements hold for every such block at $p=2$, including the block of a scalar residual Galois representation.*\n\n1.  *The functor $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Hom}}(P,-)$ is an exact equivalence from the compact block to right pseudocompact $\\mathcal E$-modules, with inverse $-\\widehat\\otimes_{\\mathcal E}P$. The algebra $\\mathcal E$ is finite over $R$.*\n\n2.  *Let $\\mathrm{CH}_R$ be the universal Cayley–Hamilton algebra for the local pseudorepresentation, and let $\\mathrm{CH}_R^{\\mathrm{tf}}$ be its maximal $\\mathcal O$-torsion-free quotient. There is an $R$-compatible identification $$\\mathrm{CH}_R^{\\mathrm{tf}}\\simeq\n     \\begin{cases}\n      (e\\mathcal E e)^{\\mathrm{op}},&\\text{in a nonsupersingular block},\\\\\n      M_2((e\\mathcal E e)^{\\mathrm{op}}),&\\text{in a supersingular block}.\n     \\end{cases}$$ The corner equivalence is induced by the Montréal functor on the quotient by the objects with trivial $\\mathop{\\mathrm{SL}}_2(\\mathbb Q_2)$-action.*\n\n3.  *An $\\mathcal E$-module finite and torsion-free over $\\mathcal O$ gives, after duality and inversion of $2$, an admissible Banach representation of finite length. At an absolutely irreducible Galois coefficient parameter there is a unique absolutely irreducible Banach object. At a sum of characters $\\gamma_1+\\gamma_2$, the simple objects have the following description. If the characters coincide, the unique simple is $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Ind}}_B^G(1\\otimes\\varepsilon^{-1})^{\\mathrm{cts}}\\otimes\\gamma_1\\circ\\det$. If their sum is $\\gamma+\\gamma\\varepsilon$, the simples are $$\\{1,\\widehat{\\mathrm{St}},\n           \\mathop{\\mathrm{Ind}}_B^G(\\varepsilon\\otimes\\varepsilon^{-1})^{\\mathrm{cts}}\\}\n           \\otimes\\gamma\\circ\\det,$$ where $\\widehat{\\mathrm{St}}$ is continuous Steinberg. If $\\gamma_1\\gamma_2^{-1}\\notin\\{1,\\varepsilon,\\varepsilon^{-1}\\}$, they are exactly $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{Ind}}_B^G(\\gamma_1\\otimes\\gamma_2\\varepsilon^{-1})^{\\mathrm{cts}},\n     \\qquad\n     \\mathop{\\mathrm{Ind}}_B^G(\\gamma_2\\otimes\\gamma_1\\varepsilon^{-1})^{\\mathrm{cts}}.$$ Here $B$ is the upper Borel, induction is unnormalized, and Galois characters are viewed as characters of $\\mathbb Q_2^\\times$ by reciprocity.",
          "source_id": "PM-010.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "The following statements hold for every such block at $p=2$, including the block of a scalar residual Galois representation.*\n\n1.  *The functor $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Hom}}(P,-)$ is an exact equivalence from the compact block to right pseudocompact $\\mathcal E$-modules, with inverse $-\\widehat\\otimes_{\\mathcal E}P$. The algebra $\\mathcal E$ is finite over $R$.*\n\n2.  *Let $\\mathrm{CH}_R$ be the universal Cayley–Hamilton algebra for the local pseudorepresentation, and let $\\mathrm{CH}_R^{\\mathrm{tf}}$ be its maximal $\\mathcal O$-torsion-free quotient. There is an $R$-compatible identification $$\\mathrm{CH}_R^{\\mathrm{tf}}\\simeq\n     \\begin{cases}\n      (e\\mathcal E e)^{\\mathrm{op}},&\\text{in a nonsupersingular block},\\\\\n      M_2((e\\mathcal E e)^{\\mathrm{op}}),&\\text{in a supersingular block}.\n     \\end{cases}$$ The corner equivalence is induced by the Montréal functor on the quotient by the objects with trivial $\\mathop{\\mathrm{SL}}_2(\\mathbb Q_2)$-action.*\n\n3.  *An $\\mathcal E$-module finite and torsion-free over $\\mathcal O$ gives, after duality and inversion of $2$, an admissible Banach representation of finite length. If their sum is $\\gamma+\\gamma\\varepsilon$, the simples are $$\\{1,\\widehat{\\mathrm{St}},\n           \\mathop{\\mathrm{Ind}}_B^G(\\varepsilon\\otimes\\varepsilon^{-1})^{\\mathrm{cts}}\\}\n           \\otimes\\gamma\\circ\\det,$$ where $\\widehat{\\mathrm{St}}$ is continuous Steinberg. If $\\gamma_1\\gamma_2^{-1}\\notin\\{1,\\varepsilon,\\varepsilon^{-1}\\}$, they are exactly $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{Ind}}_B^G(\\gamma_1\\otimes\\gamma_2\\varepsilon^{-1})^{\\mathrm{cts}},\n     \\qquad\n     \\mathop{\\mathrm{Ind}}_B^G(\\gamma_2\\otimes\\gamma_1\\varepsilon^{-1})^{\\mathrm{cts}}.$$ Here $B$ is the upper Borel, induction is unnormalized, and Galois characters are viewed as characters of $\\mathbb Q_2^\\times$ by reciprocity.",
      "explanation": "The compact block equivalence, finite endomorphism algebra, integral Cayley–Hamilton corner comparison and coefficient-fiber Banach classification apply at the dyadic prime, including scalar residual blocks.",

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-coinvariant-finite",
        "exact_quote": "The module $C_K$ is finite over $R$.         }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-principal-families",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose a nonconstant characteristic-two curve has generic local parameter $\\gamma_1+\\gamma_2$, with $\\gamma_1\\ne\\gamma_2$. After a finite extension, let $J=k'[[t]]$ be its normalization, finite over the curve ring, and $H=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Frac}}(J)$. The compact dual principal-series families over $J$ with inducing orders $$(\\gamma_1,\\gamma_2\\varepsilon^{-1}),\\qquad\n (\\gamma_2,\\gamma_1\\varepsilon^{-1})$$ correspond to finite free $J$-modules $W_1,W_2$ for $\\mathcal E$. Their central $R$-action is the given curve parameter. Their generic fibers are the two distinct simple right $\\mathcal E_H$-modules, and $$(W_1\\otimes_JH)\\otimes_{\\mathcal E_H}(C_K\\otimes_RH)\\ne0\n \\quad\\Longleftrightarrow\\quad\n (W_2\\otimes_JH)\\otimes_{\\mathcal E_H}(C_K\\otimes_RH)\\ne0.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-010.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-regular-point",
        "exact_quote": "There is a coefficient point $x:D\\to\\mathcal O'$ at which the global pseudorepresentation is that of a continuous absolutely irreducible representation $$r_x:G_{\\mathbb Q}\\longrightarrow\\mathop{\\mathrm{GL}}_2(E'),\n                    \\qquad E'=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Frac}}(\\mathcal O'),$$ after a finite coefficient extension if necessary. This representation is odd and finitely ramified. Its restriction to $G_{\\mathbb Q_2}$ is absolutely irreducible and regular de Rham, and $\\mathop{\\mathrm{WD}}(r_x|_{G_{\\mathbb Q_2}})$ is trivial on wild inertia. Consequently $r_x$ is attached to a classical cuspidal eigenform up to Tate twist.",
        "source_id": "PM-010.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-regular-point",
          "exact_quote": "There is a coefficient point $x:D\\to\\mathcal O'$ at which the global pseudorepresentation is that of a continuous absolutely irreducible representation $$r_x:G_{\\mathbb Q}\\longrightarrow\\mathop{\\mathrm{GL}}_2(E'),\n                    \\qquad E'=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Frac}}(\\mathcal O'),$$ after a finite coefficient extension if necessary. This representation is odd and finitely ramified. Its restriction to $G_{\\mathbb Q_2}$ is absolutely irreducible and regular de Rham, and $\\mathop{\\mathrm{WD}}(r_x|_{G_{\\mathbb Q_2}})$ is trivial on wild inertia. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Full completed Hecke algebra** ([source](#eq:full-hecke)): Set $$\label{eq:full-hecke} \mathbb T_2(N)= \varprojlim_k\bigl(\mathbb Z_2\otimes_{\mathbb Z}T_{\leq k}^{(2)}(N)\bigr).$$
- **Coefficient point** ([source](#pm-h-4)): A *coefficient point* of a complete local $\mathcal O$-algebra is a continuous map to the ring of integers of a finite coefficient extension.
- **Horizontal domain** ([source](#pm-h-4)): A domain over $\mathcal O$ is *horizontal* if $\varpi$ is nonzero in it.
- **Pseudorepresentation in residue characteristic two** ([source](#pm-h-4)): At residue characteristic two, a pseudorepresentation means a two-dimensional determinant law, with both trace and determinant data, in the sense of (Chenevier 2014) [[Chenevier2014](#ref-Chenevier2014)].
- **Not virtually solvable** ([source](#sec:family)): For a matrix representation, *not virtually solvable* means that the identity component of its algebraic monodromy group is not solvable.
- **Pro-modular closed trace locus** ([source](#pm-h-8)): A closed trace locus over a totally real field is *pro-modular* if it lies in the spectrum of a residual factor of the completed Hecke algebra of definite quaternionic forms, with the prescribed central character and one tame level.
- **Potentially pro-modular closed trace locus** ([source](#pm-h-8)): It is *potentially pro-modular* if this holds after a finite totally real solvable extension, split completely above $2$.
- **Coinvariant test module** ([source](#pm-h-12)): Fix a sufficiently small uniform $K\subset\mathop{\mathrm{SL}}_2(\mathbb Z_2)$, and put $$C_K=P/(\varpi P+(K-1)P).$$
- **Hilbert growth degree** ([source](#pm-h-16)): For a nonzero finite $\mathscr A$-module $M$, let $g(M)$ be the degree of the eventual Hilbert polynomial $$H_M(n)=\operatorname{length}_{\mathcal O}(M/\mathfrak a^nM).$$
- **Inflated sign type** ([source](#pm-h-17)): Let $K_0=\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}_2(\mathbb Z_2)$ and let $$\sigma:K_0\longrightarrow\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}_2(\mathbb F_2)\simeq S_3 \xrightarrow{\operatorname{sign}}\{1,-1\}$$ be the inflated sign character over $E$.

| q-trace-domain-dimension | Dimension of the completed trace domain | ≥ | $3$ | The trace image of the selected reduced framed component. |
| q-injective-quotient-growth | g(M/fM) | ≤ | $g(M)-1$ | An injective linear endomorphism with nonzero cokernel. |
| q-positive-iwasawa-rank | Iwasawa rank of the reconstructed module | > | $0$ | The fixed-determinant horizontal trace domain and its nonzero torsion-free coefficient module. |
| q-global-full-adjoint-cohomology | Dimension of global cohomology with full adjoint coefficients | ≤ | $3$ | An odd absolutely irreducible classical cuspidal representation up to Tate twist, locally absolutely irreducible and regular de Rham, with Weil–Deligne parameter trivial on wild inertia. |
| q-global-varying-determinant-embedding-dimension | Embedding dimension at the classical coefficient prime | ≤ | $3$ | Localization at the coefficient prime of the representation satisfying the tangent proposition. |
| q-dyadic-local-adjoint-quotient | Dimension of the dyadic local adjoint cohomology quotient by the finite local condition | = | $3$ | The locally absolutely irreducible representation with distinct de Rham weights used in the tangent argument. |
| q-regular-support-component-dimension | Dimension of each irreducible component of the supporting quotient | = | $four$ | The component-dimension hypothesis in the regular-support lemma. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-normalize-determinant** [claim](#stmt-lem-normalization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-normalization): Separate the determinant by global class field theory, construct a continuous square-root twist after coefficient extension, and choose a sufficiently large compatible weight outside the CM contribution. -> A normalized determinant supplied by a regular non-virtually-solvable modular representation.
- **step-build-large-trace-domain** [claim](#stmt-lem-large-family) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-large-family): Use the unrestricted framed relation estimate, pass to the closed trace image and exclude the lower-dimensional virtually solvable loci. -> A horizontal trace domain through the original point with generically absolutely irreducible finite restrictions.
- **step-propagate-potential-support** [claim](#stmt-prop-potential-support) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-potential-support): Prepare solvable fields, choose characteristic-two curves in seed and unrestricted loci, connect transverse charts and propagate support before taking a common field and level. -> Completed Hecke support of the restricted family over a solvable totally real extension split at the dyadic prime.
- **step-record-local-block-equivalence** [claim](#stmt-input-local-blocks) / [proof](#pm-h-11): Use the cited compact block equivalence, integral corner comparison and coefficient-fiber Banach classification at their stated scope. -> The finite endomorphism algebra and exact reconstruction functors.
- **step-assemble-diagonal-module** [claim](#stmt-prop-family) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-family): Make restricted trace generators integral using the trace-of-powers recurrence, factor equal local actions through the diagonal coefficient ring and remove coefficient torsion from the base-changed faithful module. -> A nonzero finite torsion-free diagonal coefficient module with admissible product reconstruction.
- **step-control-coinvariants** [claim](#stmt-lem-coinvariant-finite) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-coinvariant-finite): Apply residual finite length, admissibility of smooth simples and compact Nakayama; commute reconstruction with the cokernels defining coinvariants. -> Finite coinvariant coefficients and compatible single-factor, product and field-base-change formulas.
- **step-make-corner-full** [claim](#stmt-lem-full-corner) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-full-corner): Show that the quotient with trivial determinant-one action is finite over the integer coefficients and therefore has special-fiber support only at the closed point. -> Fullness of the noncharacter corner at the curve's generic point.
- **step-compare-principal-series-families** [claim](#stmt-lem-principal-families) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-principal-families): Construct mixed-characteristic principal-series families, retain distinct corner labels through specialization and compare inverse stabilizer characters on determinant-one double cosets. -> Distinct generic simple modules with simultaneous coinvariant survival.
- **step-prove-uniform-curve-survival** [claim](#stmt-lem-curve-support) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-curve-support): Exclude repeated characters on a nonconstant curve and combine the unique-simple case with simultaneous principal-series survival and a nonzero semisimple head. -> Nonvanishing after tensoring with every simple right module.
- **step-descend-admissibility** [claim](#stmt-prop-diagonal) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-curve-support-2): Assume single-factor coinvariants have nonconstant curve support, apply uniform survival successively across equal local factors and contradict the finite-dimensional product coinvariants. -> A nonzero torsion-free admissible single-factor object with commuting trace-domain action.
- **step-bound-growth-drop** [claim](#stmt-lem-growth-drop) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-growth-drop): Use Artin–Rees to compare filtered lengths under an injective endomorphism, then bound growth of cyclic torsion quotients through nonzero initial forms. -> Growth decreases under nonzero injective quotients, while maximal growth forces positive rank.
- **step-force-positive-rank** [claim](#stmt-prop-positive-rank) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-positive-rank): Choose a coefficient point outside exceptional reducible parameters, specialize along a saturated prime chain and compare growth with the infinite-dimensional torsion-free terminal fiber. -> Positive Iwasawa rank.
- **step-capture-type** [claim](#stmt-lem-type-capture) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-type-capture): Dualize a nonzero Iwasawa-linear functional and use polynomial approximation of matrix coefficients with prescribed central degree. -> A nonzero finite-dimensional algebraic-type multiplicity space carrying the trace-domain action.
- **step-exclude-reducible-type-parameters** [claim](#stmt-lem-type-irreducible) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-type-irreducible): Use the unipotent action of the sign-twisted algebraic type to exclude continuous induction and characters; split the finite-dimensional pullback extension to exclude Steinberg. -> Absolute irreducibility of the local Galois parameter.
- **step-produce-regular-classical-point** [claim](#stmt-prop-regular-point) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-regular-point): Extract a common coefficient eigenvector, pass the type to a Banach simple and to definite forms upstairs, then apply classical comparison, depth-zero local compatibility and regular modularity. -> An auxiliary classical point with regular de Rham dyadic restriction and Weil–Deligne parameter trivial on wild inertia.
- **step-evaluate-classical-dyadic-level** [claim](#stmt-prop-hecke-level) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-hecke-level): Evaluate forms on the formal ordinary tower, prove rational density of positive-weight tame forms and extend eigenvalue evaluation by integral Hecke contraction bounds. -> A continuous coefficient point of the full odd-tame-level Hecke algebra.
- **step-extend-continuous-twists** [claim](#stmt-prop-hecke-twists) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-hecke-twists): Prove finite-character stability using dense classical points and reducedness, then force the universal character deformation to factor through Hecke support by Weierstrass preparation. -> The twisted determinant at the same tame level.
- **step-bound-full-adjoint-tangent** [claim](#stmt-prop-tangent-bound) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-tangent-bound): Use tame Weil–Deligne behavior to establish the CM qualification for adjoint Selmer vanishing, compute local finite-condition quotients and apply characteristic-zero tangent comparison. -> The full varying-determinant cohomological and embedding-dimension bounds.
- **step-propagate-regular-support** [claim](#stmt-lem-regular-support) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-regular-support): Compare localized component dimension with embedding dimension, deduce regularity and vanishing of the localized supporting kernel, then use the domain quotient to remove localization. -> Factorization of the entire domain quotient through the supporting quotient.
- **step-recover-original-hecke-point** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-regular-support-2): Place the auxiliary classical point in the precise Hecke algebra, apply regular-support factorization to the varying-determinant deformation ring, reverse the original twist and descend integral coefficients by topological generation. -> A continuous Hecke coefficient point over the original integer coefficients with the required Frobenius values.

          "exact_quote": "The following statements hold for every such block at $p=2$, including the block of a scalar residual Galois representation.*\n\n1.  *The functor $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Hom}}(P,-)$ is an exact equivalence from the compact block to right pseudocompact $\\mathcal E$-modules, with inverse $-\\widehat\\otimes_{\\mathcal E}P$. The algebra $\\mathcal E$ is finite over $R$.*\n\n2.  *Let $\\mathrm{CH}_R$ be the universal Cayley–Hamilton algebra for the local pseudorepresentation, and let $\\mathrm{CH}_R^{\\mathrm{tf}}$ be its maximal $\\mathcal O$-torsion-free quotient. There is an $R$-compatible identification $$\\mathrm{CH}_R^{\\mathrm{tf}}\\simeq\n     \\begin{cases}\n      (e\\mathcal E e)^{\\mathrm{op}},&\\text{in a nonsupersingular block},\\\\\n      M_2((e\\mathcal E e)^{\\mathrm{op}}),&\\text{in a supersingular block}.\n     \\end{cases}$$ The corner equivalence is induced by the Montréal functor on the quotient by the objects with trivial $\\mathop{\\mathrm{SL}}_2(\\mathbb Q_2)$-action.*\n\n3.  *An $\\mathcal E$-module finite and torsion-free over $\\mathcal O$ gives, after duality and inversion of $2$, an admissible Banach representation of finite length. At an absolutely irreducible Galois coefficient parameter there is a unique absolutely irreducible Banach object. At a sum of characters $\\gamma_1+\\gamma_2$, the simple objects have the following description. If the characters coincide, the unique simple is $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Ind}}_B^G(1\\otimes\\varepsilon^{-1})^{\\mathrm{cts}}\\otimes\\gamma_1\\circ\\det$. If their sum is $\\gamma+\\gamma\\varepsilon$, the simples are $$\\{1,\\widehat{\\mathrm{St}},\n           \\mathop{\\mathrm{Ind}}_B^G(\\varepsilon\\otimes\\varepsilon^{-1})^{\\mathrm{cts}}\\}\n           \\otimes\\gamma\\circ\\det,$$ where $\\widehat{\\mathrm{St}}$ is continuous Steinberg. If $\\gamma_1\\gamma_2^{-1}\\notin\\{1,\\varepsilon,\\varepsilon^{-1}\\}$, they are exactly $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{Ind}}_B^G(\\gamma_1\\otimes\\gamma_2\\varepsilon^{-1})^{\\mathrm{cts}},\n     \\qquad\n     \\mathop{\\mathrm{Ind}}_B^G(\\gamma_2\\otimes\\gamma_1\\varepsilon^{-1})^{\\mathrm{cts}}.$$ Here $B$ is the upper Borel, induction is unnormalized, and Galois characters are viewed as characters of $\\mathbb Q_2^\\times$ by reciprocity.",
          "source_id": "PM-010.1"
        }
      ],

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-completed-hecke-occurrence**: Every representation satisfying the global hypotheses occurs in the full completed Hecke algebra at some odd tame level containing its odd ramified primes. The coefficient point recovers both Frobenius traces and determinants away from the level.
- **invariant-diagonal-admissibility**: Product admissibility yields a nonzero torsion-free admissible object for a single local factor when all central local parameter actions coincide and the coefficient finiteness hypotheses hold.
- **invariant-continuous-twists**: Continuous cyclotomic character twists of a Hecke coefficient determinant remain coefficient determinants of the same odd tame-level algebra.
- **invariant-regular-support-factorization**: A common coefficient point with the stated embedding-dimension bound forces the domain quotient to factor through the equidimensional Hecke-type quotient.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-classical-modularity-at-original-point**: Infer that the original representation comes from a classical cuspidal eigenform. -> Occurrence in a completed varying-weight spectrum does not supply classical vectors at the original coefficient point. The regular de Rham point constructed in the proof is an auxiliary point.
- **anti-prescribed-or-minimal-tame-level**: Use the theorem to compute the minimal tame level or guarantee occurrence at a prescribed set of tame primes. -> The theorem permits an existentially chosen level, and the proof enlarges the allowed tame ramification set to accommodate the auxiliary classical point.
- **anti-global-residual-confusion**: Extend the occurrence theorem to globally reducible representations because reducible residual representations are included. -> The permission concerns residual data. Absolute irreducibility of the characteristic-zero global representation remains a hypothesis.
- **anti-automatic-factor-restriction**: Deduce admissibility for a single group factor from product admissibility alone. -> The proof needs the same parameter and the same curve coinvariant test in every factor. Product admissibility without diagonal parameter equality does not meet the proposition's hypotheses.
- **anti-modular-point-alone**: Propagate Hecke support to an entire deformation family merely because the family contains a modular point. -> The factorization argument also uses the ambient embedding-dimension bound, the dimensions of the supporting quotient's components, and the domain property of the family quotient.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The occurrence theorem concerns infinite completed algebras and continuous Galois representations. Its proof selects coefficient points by density and avoids closed loci; it does not specify a finite constructive procedure for producing the level and coefficient homomorphism. In the proof of the large-family lemma: “The union of these finitely many closed loci cannot contain the generic point of $D$, whose dimension is at least three.” The conclusion then requires further existential choices of a base-change field, a regular coefficient point, and a sufficiently divisible tame level. Finite arithmetic examples would not decide these existence and support assertions.

            "exact_quote": "Let $E/\\mathbb Q_2$ be finite, with ring of integers $\\mathcal O_E$, and let $r:G_{\\mathbb Q}\\longrightarrow\\mathop{\\mathrm{GL}}_2(E)$ be continuous and absolutely irreducible.",
            "source_object": "stmt-thm-main"

          {

            "exact_quote": "Suppose that $r$ is unramified outside finitely many finite primes and is odd: $\\det r(c)=-1$ for complex conjugation $c$.",

          }

        "id": "invariant-completed-hecke-occurrence",
        "quantifiers": [
          {
            "anchor": "stmt-thm-main",
            "exact_quote": "Then there exist an odd positive integer $N$, divisible by every odd prime where $r$ ramifies, and a continuous $\\mathbb Z_2$-algebra homomorphism $$\\lambda:\\mathbb T_2(N)\\longrightarrow\\mathcal O_E$$ such that, for every prime $\\ell\\nmid2N$, $$\\lambda(T_\\ell)=\\mathop{\\mathrm{tr}}r(\\operatorname{Frob}_\\ell),\\qquad\n \\lambda(\\ell S_\\ell)=\\det r(\\operatorname{Frob}_\\ell).$$",
            "source_object": "stmt-thm-main"
          }
        ],
        "regime": "Continuous, odd, absolutely irreducible dyadic representations over a finite coefficient field, with finite ramification; scalar and reducible semisimplified residual representations are allowed.",

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The occurrence theorem supplies an existential mathematical specification for a continuous homomorphism, without a finite procedure for computing that homomorphism from a representation. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The admissible Banach representations and locally algebraic vectors are arithmetic representation-theoretic objects; the paper supplies no simulation state, evolution rule, or finite approximation contract. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Evaluation of classical eigenforms on the completed Hecke algebra establishes continuity, but does not specify a computational interface for extracting eigenvalues or representing the completed algebra finitely. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Potential support is obtained through existence of a solvable totally real extension and a common tame level. The statement gives no effective search bound or terminating procedure for selecting them. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Finiteness of coinvariants over a complete coefficient ring is an algebraic property. It does not supply a finite encoding, generators suitable for computation, or operations on an encoded module. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The growth comparison concerns filtered modules over an Iwasawa algebra. It provides no hardware execution model, parallel decomposition, or resource estimate for an accelerator. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Continuous cyclotomic twisting preserves Hecke coefficient points under the stated arithmetic hypotheses. It introduces no cryptographic construction, adversary model, or hardness assumption. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The trace family is a deformation-theoretic domain containing a coefficient point. Its dimension bounds do not define training data, a statistical model, a learning objective, or a generalisation guarantee. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The tangent bound controls arithmetic cohomology and local embedding dimension at a qualified classical point. It supplies neither an optimisation objective nor a feasible-set algorithm. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Passing from a product of local groups to a single factor requires identical central parameters and compact admissibility. This categorical comparison provides no distributed protocol or communication guarantee. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The regular-support lemma is relevant to foundational formalisation in commutative algebra, but its complete local rings and dimension hypotheses do not yield a finite executable verification contract in the supplied paper. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-global-representation-hypotheses** (hypothesis): The occurrence theorem requires a continuous absolutely irreducible representation over a finite dyadic coefficient field, with odd determinant and finite ramification. Blocked use: ['Apply the theorem to an even or globally reducible representation.', 'Extend the conclusion to representations of a different dimension or base field.']
- **limit-residual-versus-global-irreducibility** (formal_scope): Allowance for scalar or reducible residual data does not remove absolute irreducibility of the characteristic-zero global representation. Blocked use: ['Treat permission for reducible semisimplified residue as permission for a reducible input representation.']
- **limit-completed-occurrence-not-classical-input** (formal_scope): The original representation is placed in the completed Hecke spectrum. Classical modularity is obtained for an auxiliary regular point and is not asserted for the unrestricted original point. Blocked use: ['Extract a classical eigenform for the original representation solely from completed-Hecke occurrence.']
- **limit-existential-tame-level** (nonconstructive): The proof selects a sufficiently divisible odd tame level accommodating both the original family and the auxiliary classical representation. It gives neither a minimal-level conclusion nor an effective bound for a finite search. Blocked use: ['Guarantee occurrence at exactly the original ramified tame primes.', 'Present the existence theorem as a terminating minimal-level computation.']
- **limit-normalization-and-reversal** (hypothesis): Determinant normalization permits a finite coefficient extension and a continuous cyclotomic twist. It preserves oddness, but the normalized representation must be distinguished from the original representation until the twist is reversed. Blocked use: ['Identify the normalized determinant with the original determinant without accounting for the twist.', 'Assume an arbitrary determinant already has the prescribed algebraic normalization.']
- **limit-trace-family-selection** (nonconstructive): The large-family argument uses a reduced horizontal component through the prescribed representation and coefficient-point density outside closed exceptional loci. These existence arguments do not provide finite encodings of the component or the selected point. Blocked use: ['Compute a deformation component or a non-virtually-solvable coefficient point from the dimension bound alone.']
- **limit-potential-support-base-change** (formal_scope): Potential support is established after a solvable totally real Galois extension of even degree completely split at the dyadic prime. Support over that extension is an intermediate conclusion requiring further arguments to recover support over the original field. Blocked use: ['Replace the permitted base change by an arbitrary extension.', 'Claim occurrence over the original field directly from potential support.']
- **limit-integral-block-center** (formal_scope): The local argument uses a finite map from the pseudodeformation ring and the integral Cayley–Hamilton comparison. It does not identify the pseudodeformation ring with the integral center. Blocked use: ['Replace the finite coefficient map by an integral center isomorphism without an additional argument.']
- **limit-diagonal-admissibility-preconditions** (hypothesis): Single-factor admissibility requires a horizontal complete local domain, a finite continuous local parameter map, a nonzero finite torsion-free coefficient module, equal central parameter actions and admissible product reconstruction. Blocked use: ['Infer single-factor admissibility from product admissibility alone.', 'Discard coefficient torsion-freeness or equality of local parameter actions.']
- **limit-curve-generic-fibers** (edge_case): The fullness and uniform-survival arguments concern generic points of nonconstant characteristic-two curves. Uniform survival additionally requires a nonzero coinvariant fiber and a sufficiently large splitting field. Blocked use: ['Apply generic fullness at the closed residual point.', 'Infer survival from a zero coinvariant fiber or an unsplit parameter without the required extension.']
- **limit-principal-family-distinctness** (hypothesis): The principal-series comparison assumes distinct generic character constituents. Its simultaneous-vanishing conclusion does not independently cover a repeated-character parameter. Blocked use: ['Apply the distinct-simple principal-series construction to coincident generic characters.']
- **limit-growth-drop-injectivity** (hypothesis): The growth-drop lemma requires an injective linear endomorphism and a nonzero cokernel. Its maximal-growth conclusion establishes positive rank, rather than absence of every torsion submodule. Blocked use: ['Apply the growth decrease to a noninjective endomorphism.', 'Strengthen positive rank to torsion-freeness of an arbitrary finite module.']
- **limit-type-capture-existential-weight** (nonconstructive): Type capture proves that some algebraic representation of the prescribed central degree occurs. Polynomial approximation supplies existence without a finite search bound for the algebraic type. Blocked use: ['Treat type capture as a bounded enumeration algorithm for an algebraic weight.', 'Replace the prescribed smooth type by an arbitrary type while retaining the local irreducibility conclusion.']
- **limit-auxiliary-regular-point** (formal_scope): The regular de Rham and wild-inertia conclusions belong to the auxiliary coefficient point. They do not impose those local properties on every point of the trace family. Blocked use: ['Transfer regular de Rhamness or trivial wild inertia from the auxiliary point to the original input representation.']
- **limit-classical-evaluation-scope** (hypothesis): The classical evaluation proposition assumes an odd tame level satisfying its lower bound and a positive-weight classical cuspidal eigenform whose level divides the permitted tame level times a power of the dyadic prime. Blocked use: ['Apply the proposition directly to an arbitrary continuous Galois representation.', 'Remove the classical cuspidality or level-divisibility assumptions.']
- **limit-continuous-twist-scope** (hypothesis): Twist stability concerns continuous characters of the dyadic units valued in a finite coefficient extension, starting from a continuous Hecke coefficient point at the specified odd tame level. Blocked use: ['Infer stability at the same tame level for an arbitrary global character with additional odd conductor.', 'Drop continuity or the finite coefficient-extension requirement.']
- **limit-qualified-adjoint-tangent-bound** (hypothesis): The tangent bound is proved at an odd absolutely irreducible classical cuspidal representation up to Tate twist, with locally absolutely irreducible regular de Rham restriction and Weil–Deligne representation trivial on wild inertia. Blocked use: ['Apply the tangent bound to an arbitrary point of the unrestricted family.', 'Omit the local irreducibility, distinct-weight or wild-inertia conditions from the stated argument.']
- **limit-regular-support-domain-and-dimensions** (hypothesis): Support factorization requires quotients of a common complete local coefficient algebra, a domain quotient, a shared continuous coefficient point and the precise component-dimension and embedding-dimension hypotheses. Blocked use: ['Propagate support from a shared modular point alone.', 'Apply the domain cancellation argument to a quotient with zero divisors.', 'Replace local dimension hypotheses by observations at finitely many sampled points.']
- **limit-coefficient-descent-requires-closedness** (formal_scope): Recovery of the original coefficient ring uses integrality of the original traces and determinants, topological generation by the Hecke elements and closedness of the original integer coefficients inside the enlarged coefficient ring. Blocked use: ['Infer descent of the entire homomorphism merely from the existence of a point over an enlarged coefficient field.']

  "status": "none"
}

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


Every continuous, odd, absolutely irreducible two-dimensional 2-adic representation of $`G_{\mathbb Q}`$ that is unramified outside finitely many finite primes occurs in the full completed Hecke algebra at some odd tame level. The level may contain auxiliary tame primes, and scalar and reducible residual representations are included. This is a completed-Hecke occurrence result, with no de Rham hypothesis; it does not assert classical modularity.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### The full Hecke algebra and the theorem

Congruences between modular forms of different weights assemble their Hecke eigenvalues into a single $p$-adic algebra. Its points need not come from classical forms of one weight. The resulting modularity question asks whether a Galois representation occurs in this larger spectrum, even when no Hodge-theoretic condition is imposed at $p$. We prove this occurrence statement for every odd, absolutely irreducible two-dimensional representation over $\mathbb Q$ at $p=2$.

<a id="eq:full-hecke"></a>
<a id="eq:frobenius"></a>
Fix an odd positive integer $N$. For $i\geq1$, let $M_i(\Gamma_1(N))$ denote the space of all complex holomorphic modular forms of weight $i$, including Eisenstein forms. Let $T_{\leq k}^{(2)}(N)$ be the $\mathbb Z$-algebra acting on $\bigoplus_{i=1}^{k}M_i(\Gamma_1(N))$ generated by $T_\ell$ and $\ell S_\ell$ for primes $\ell\nmid2N$. On weight $i$, the operator $S_\ell$ is $\ell^{i-2}\langle\ell\rangle$. Set $$\label{eq:full-hecke}
 \mathbb T_2(N)=
 \varprojlim_k\bigl(\mathbb Z_2\otimes_{\mathbb Z}T_{\leq k}^{(2)}(N)\bigr).$$ Each finite-stage algebra has its $2$-adic topology, and $\mathbb T_2(N)$ has the resulting inverse-limit topology. We fix the Frobenius convention in which a classical eigenform has characteristic polynomial $$\label{eq:frobenius}
 Z^2-T_\ell Z+\ell S_\ell$$ at a good prime, as in (OpenAI 2026b, Equation (2.2)) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Dim](#ref-Dim)].

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *Let $E/\mathbb Q_2$ be finite, with ring of integers $\mathcal O_E$, and let $r:G_{\mathbb Q}\longrightarrow\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}_2(E)$ be continuous and absolutely irreducible. Suppose that $r$ is unramified outside finitely many finite primes and is odd: $\det r(c)=-1$ for complex conjugation $c$. Then there exist an odd positive integer $N$, divisible by every odd prime where $r$ ramifies, and a continuous $\mathbb Z_2$-algebra homomorphism $$\lambda:\mathbb T_2(N)\longrightarrow\mathcal O_E$$ such that, for every prime $\ell\nmid2N$, $$\lambda(T_\ell)=\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}r(\operatorname{Frob}_\ell),\qquad
 \lambda(\ell S_\ell)=\det r(\operatorname{Frob}_\ell).$$*

</div>

We call this conclusion *pro-modularity*. No condition on the semisimplified reduction of $r$ is imposed. In particular, the theorem includes scalar residual representations. The compact image of $r$ preserves an $\mathcal O_E$-lattice, so its traces and determinants are integral.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### Context and main ideas

The Fontaine–Mazur conjecture relates Galois representations satisfying geometric local conditions to algebraic geometry. In its odd, two-dimensional regular form over $\mathbb Q$, the expected conclusion is classical cuspidal modularity up to a Tate twist (Fontaine and Mazur 1995; Pan 2022) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[FM1995](#ref-FM1995), [Pan2022](#ref-Pan2022)]. Removing the local condition leads to a different question: occurrence of the representation in a completed Hecke algebra. Emerton records this expectation for the full varying-weight algebra in (Emerton 2011b, Conjecture 2.12) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[Emerton2011Families](#ref-Emerton2011Families)]. That formulation prescribes the allowed tame primes. Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> proves dyadic occurrence for absolutely irreducible representations while allowing auxiliary primes in the tame level.

Skinner and Wiles developed pro-modularity arguments through ordinary families in the residually reducible setting (Skinner and Wiles 1999, Introduction and Section 4.1) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[SkinnerWiles1999](#ref-SkinnerWiles1999)]. Emerton’s local–global compatibility work separates pro-modularity from the production of classical vectors (Emerton 2011a, sec. 1.2) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[Emerton2011](#ref-Emerton2011)]; the pro-modularity theorem recorded there assumes $p>2$, residual irreducibility, and local residual restrictions. Pan extends the family method to the residually reducible nonordinary setting at odd primes: ordinary loci supply modular points, and patching at one-dimensional primes propagates Hecke support (Pan 2022, Introduction and Theorem 4.1.7) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[Pan2022](#ref-Pan2022)]. His classical modularity theorems retain regular de Rham hypotheses. These precedents suggest placing an unrestricted representation in a family and finding a useful geometric point elsewhere in that family.

The local representation theory used here comes from Colmez’s $p$-adic Langlands correspondence (Colmez 2010) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[Colmez2010](#ref-Colmez2010)], in the all-prime form of Colmez, Dospinescu, and Paškūnas (Colmez, Dospinescu, and Paškūnas 2014, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[CDP2014](#ref-CDP2014)]. Paškūnas and Tung supply the integral block finiteness and Cayley–Hamilton descriptions needed at $2$, including scalar residual blocks (Paškūnas and Tung 2021, Theorems 1.2–1.4 and Section 4.1) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[PaskunasTung2021](#ref-PaskunasTung2021)]. Their results control local parameters but do not by themselves place a global representation on Hecke support.

After a continuous cyclotomic twist, we place the original representation in a fixed-determinant trace family $D$ of dimension at least three. The curve and connectedness arguments of (OpenAI 2026a) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[FM2](#ref-FM2)] put this family on Hecke support after solvable totally real base change, as developed in Section <a href="#sec:family" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:family">2</a>. The main task is then to find a regular de Rham point on $D$, from which Hecke support can be transferred to the whole family over $\mathbb Q$.

Two constructions proved here produce that point. We choose the base-change field to split completely at $2$, so that all dyadic restrictions of the family have the same local Galois parameter. Section <a href="#sec:diagonal" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:diagonal">3</a> passes through the local block equivalences to construct an object for one copy of $\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}_2(\mathbb Q_2)$ from an admissible representation of the product of dyadic groups. Equality of the parameters ensures that a failure of admissibility would persist on a characteristic-two curve in every factor, contradicting the admissibility of the product representation. This comparison includes scalar residual blocks. Section <a href="#sec:regular-point" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:regular-point">4</a> then uses a chain of prime specializations in $D$ to force maximal growth, and hence positive Iwasawa rank, for the one-factor object. Polynomial approximation detects a locally algebraic depth-zero supercuspidal type, yielding a regular de Rham point $x$. The regular Fontaine–Mazur theorem (OpenAI 2026a, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[FM2](#ref-FM2)] makes $x$ classical up to Tate twist, and Section <a href="#sec:hecke" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:hecke">5</a> puts its classical packet and continuous cyclotomic twists in the precise algebra <a href="#eq:full-hecke" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:full-hecke">[eq:full-hecke]</a>.

The final passage uses the dimension theorem of (OpenAI 2026b, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[Dim](#ref-Dim)] and the adjoint Selmer vanishing theorem of Newton and Thorne (Newton and Thorne 2023, Theorem 5.4) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[NewtonThorne2023](#ref-NewtonThorne2023)]. At $x$, the global deformation problem with varying determinant has tangent dimension at most three, while a dimension-four integral Hecke component has local dimension three. Equality makes the ambient local ring regular and forces its Hecke kernel to vanish there. Since $D$ is a domain through $x$, the same kernel vanishes on all of $D$. Section <a href="#sec:conclusion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:conclusion">6</a> carries out this comparison and recovers the original coefficient point. The Hodge-theoretic condition is needed only at the auxiliary point.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### Coefficient and deformation conventions

We allow finite extensions of coefficient fields during the proof. Write $\mathcal O$ for the current ring of integers, $\varpi$ for a uniformizer, and $k$ for the residue field. A *coefficient point* of a complete local $\mathcal O$-algebra is a continuous map to the ring of integers of a finite coefficient extension. Its kernel is a characteristic-zero prime; the corresponding integral quotient has dimension one. A domain over $\mathcal O$ is *horizontal* if $\varpi$ is nonzero in it.

At residue characteristic two, a pseudorepresentation means a two-dimensional determinant law, with both trace and determinant data, in the sense of (Chenevier 2014) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[Chenevier2014](#ref-Chenevier2014)]. Thus no division by $2$ is implicit in an integral deformation ring. All global deformation problems have a fixed finite ramification set containing $2$ and allow ramification at infinity. Trace images and support containments are closed images and set-theoretic containments; the domains used for the family are reduced.

For the local categories and completed definite forms we use the normalization of (OpenAI 2026a, sec. 2) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[FM2](#ref-FM2)]. If the fixed global determinant is $\chi$, the central character is $\psi=\chi\varepsilon$, where $\varepsilon$ is the cyclotomic character. The local block parameter is the Galois parameter twisted by $\varepsilon$, of determinant $\psi\varepsilon=\chi\varepsilon^2$. Group duals have the contragredient action; commuting coefficient and Hecke operators act by transposition. The conversion to the Frobenius identity <a href="#eq:frobenius" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:frobenius">[eq:frobenius]</a> is made explicitly when passing to classical Hecke points.

<a id="sec:family"></a>
### A trace family with potential automorphic support

We first place the given representation in a sufficiently large fixed-determinant family. We then put that entire family on completed Hecke support after a totally real base change. The representation itself need not have an algebraic weight. The distinction between its residual representation and an auxiliary representation supplying the determinant will be essential in the support argument. For a matrix representation, *not virtually solvable* means that the identity component of its algebraic monodromy group is not solvable. This property is unchanged on restriction to a finite index subgroup.

<a id="pm-h-6"></a>
#### Normalizing the determinant

We will use the following twist property, proved in Proposition <a href="#prop:hecke-twists" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:hecke-twists">18</a>: at an odd tame level $N\geq5$, twisting a Hecke coefficient point by any continuous character $\eta:\mathbb Z_2^\times\to\mathcal O'^\times$ gives another point of the same Hecke algebra. At a good prime $\ell$, this multiplies the trace by $\eta(\varepsilon(\operatorname{Frob}_\ell))$ and the determinant by its square. Thus it suffices to prove the theorem after any such twist of $r$, and to undo the twist at the end.

<a id="stmt-lem-normalization"></a>
<div id="lem:normalization" class="lemma">

**Lemma 2**. *After a finite coefficient extension and a twist through $\varepsilon$, one may arrange $$\det r=\chi=\delta\varepsilon^w,$$ where $\delta$ has finite order and is unramified at $2$. The integer $w$ may be chosen arbitrarily large in one parity class. It may in particular be chosen so that $\chi$ is the determinant of a regular cuspidal modular representation that is not virtually solvable. Oddness is preserved. The character $\psi=\chi\varepsilon$, viewed on $\mathbb Z_2^\times$ by local reciprocity, is $z\mapsto z^{w+1}$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-normalization"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-normalization</summary>

*Proof.* Global class field theory separates the dyadic part of $\det r$ from its odd conductor part: $$\det r=\delta\,\alpha\circ\varepsilon,
 \qquad \alpha:\mathbb Z_2^\times\longrightarrow\mathcal O^\times.$$ Here $\delta$ has finite order and odd conductor. Indeed, at an odd prime the pro-$\ell$ group of principal units has finite image in $\mathcal O^\times$, whose open subgroup is pro-$2$; only finitely many primes occur. The remaining unramified global character is trivial by the class field theory of $\mathbb Q$.

Choose $w$ with $(-1)^w=\alpha(-1)$. Write $\mathbb Z_2^\times=\{\pm1\}\times5^{\mathbb Z_2}$. The value $\alpha(5)$ reduces to one, since a pro-$2$ group has trivial image in $k^\times$. After finite coefficient extension choose $$u^2=5^w\alpha(5)^{-1}.$$ The element $u$ is integral and reduces to one. Its powers $u^{2^n}$ tend to one, so $a\mapsto u^a$ defines a continuous character of $\mathbb Z_2$. Define $\beta(-1)=1$ and $\beta(5)=u$. Then $\alpha(z)\beta(z)^2=z^w$ for every $z\in\mathbb Z_2^\times$. Replacing $r$ by $r\otimes(\beta\circ\varepsilon)$ gives the asserted determinant. Since $\beta(-1)=1$, complex conjugation and oddness are unchanged. Local reciprocity identifies $\varepsilon$ on dyadic units with the identity character; $\delta$ is unramified there.

For completeness, the auxiliary modular representation can be chosen without imposing the residual representation of $r$. Oddness gives $\delta(-1)=(-1)^{w+1}$. Fix an odd level divisible by the conductor of $\delta$, and enlarge it to a fine level. The dimension formula (Cohen and Oesterlé 1977, sec. III, Théorème 1) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[CohenOesterle1977](#ref-CohenOesterle1977)] for cusp forms with this character grows linearly with the weight $w+1$ along the indicated parity class. The CM contribution at this fixed level is bounded independently of the weight. To see the latter assertion, a CM eigenform comes from a Hecke character of an imaginary quadratic field $K$; its level is $|\operatorname{disc}K|\operatorname{Norm}(\mathfrak f)$, where $\mathfrak f$ is the character conductor (Ribet 1977, Theorem (3.4), Corollary (3.5), and Theorem (4.5)) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[Ribet1977](#ref-Ribet1977)]. Only finitely many pairs $(K,\mathfrak f)$ occur at the fixed level. For a fixed infinity type the characters with conductor dividing a fixed ideal form, when nonempty, a torsor under a finite ray class character group. Oldform multiplicities are bounded by the fixed level as well. Thus a sufficiently large $w$ gives a non-CM cuspidal eigenform of determinant $\delta\varepsilon^w$. Its representation is regular and not virtually solvable. Indeed its restriction to every finite index subgroup is semisimple; if its connected algebraic monodromy were solvable, its unipotent radical would act trivially, so that connected group would be a torus. An open subgroup would then have abelian image, which Ribet’s CM criterion excludes (Ribet 1977, Propositions (4.2) and (4.4)) <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[Ribet1977](#ref-Ribet1977)]. ◻

</details>

We henceforth use the normalized $r$ and enlarge the finite coefficient ring $\mathcal O$ whenever necessary. Every enlargement is finite. When a domain splits after coefficient extension, choose a component through the specified coefficient point. Finite flat scalar extension makes its minimal prime contract to the old minimal prime, so its dimension is unchanged. Reduced support containments also persist under this operation. Thus the original coefficient point and the dimension bounds below are retained.

<a id="pm-h-7"></a>
#### The dimension of the trace family

<a id="stmt-lem-large-family"></a>
<div id="lem:large-family" class="lemma">

**Lemma 3**. *Fix a finite ramification set $S$ containing $2$, infinity, and the ramified primes of the normalized $r$. There is a reduced horizontal framed deformation component $B$ of determinant $\chi$ through $r$. Its completed trace image $D$ is a complete local $\mathcal O$-domain, contains the coefficient point of $r$, and satisfies $$\dim B\ge6,\qquad \dim D\ge3.$$ Moreover, $B$ has a coefficient point whose representation is not virtually solvable. Consequently the restriction of this family to any finite extension of $\mathbb Q$ is generically absolutely irreducible.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-large-family"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-large-family</summary>

*Proof.* Choose a stable lattice of $r$ and let $R^{\square,\chi}_{\bar r,S}$ be its unrestricted framed deformation ring with determinant $\chi$. The residue here is the actual lattice reduction; no semisimplicity or irreducibility is imposed on it. These rings and their maps to fixed-determinant pseudodeformation rings exist under Mazur’s finiteness condition, which holds for $G_{\mathbb Q,S}$ (OpenAI 2026a, Proposition 3.1) <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[FM2](#ref-FM2)].

Complete at the characteristic-zero point of $r$. Its real local framed deformation ring is smooth of dimension two: an odd involution has eigenvalues $1,-1$, and its conjugacy orbit has stabilizer the two-dimensional diagonal torus. For the global ring over this real local ring, the relative relation estimate of (OpenAI 2026a, Proposition 3.6) <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[FM2](#ref-FM2)], with no imposed dyadic condition, is $$g-r_{\mathrm{rel}}\ge
 3-h^0\bigl((\mathop{\mathrm{ad}}^0r)^\vee(1)\bigr)=3.$$ The equality follows from absolute irreducibility. In characteristic zero the trace pairing identifies $\mathop{\mathrm{ad}}^0r$ with its dual; a nonzero invariant in its cyclotomic twist would give a nonzero map $r\to r\otimes\varepsilon$. Such a map is an isomorphism, whereas its determinants would require $\varepsilon^2=1$. Every component of the completed local ring therefore has dimension at least $2+3=5$. The dimension comparison at a coefficient point in (OpenAI 2026a, Lemma 3.4) <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[FM2](#ref-FM2)] adds one on returning to a horizontal integral component. Choose such a component through $r$ and give it the reduced structure; its ring $B$ has dimension at least six.

Let $R^{\mathrm{ps},\chi}_{S}$ be the global pseudodeformation ring of the semisimplified lattice reduction, and set $$D=R^{\mathrm{ps},\chi}_{S}/
       \ker\bigl(R^{\mathrm{ps},\chi}_{S}\longrightarrow B\bigr).$$ Write $T(g)\in D$ for its universal trace at $g$. The ring $D$ is a horizontal complete local domain. Its image in $B$ is closed: complete local rings with finite residue field are compact, and their continuous images in Hausdorff rings are closed. The generic representation on $B$ is absolutely irreducible, since the irreducible locus contains $r$. Forgetting its single frame loses at most three dimensions, by (OpenAI 2026a, Lemma 3.2) <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[FM2](#ref-FM2)]. Hence $\dim D\ge\dim B-3\ge3$.

We justify the last assertion by bounding the trace loci of the virtually solvable representations. The reducible locus is closed and proper on $D$, because it does not contain $r$. An absolutely irreducible virtually solvable two-dimensional representation is induced from a character of a quadratic field, or has finite projective image. This follows by considering the connected solvable algebraic subgroup: a noncentral torus has two eigenlines permuted by the whole group, while a central connected subgroup gives finite projective image. A nontrivial connected unipotent subgroup would have a unique invariant line and force reducibility.

Only finitely many quadratic fields $K$ can occur. The corresponding quadratic self-twist is unramified outside $S$, so this is Hermite–Minkowski with bounded local degree. For each $K$, the maximal abelian pro-$2$ quotient of $G_{K,S}$ has rank at most two: class field theory bounds its free part by the dyadic unit groups, of total rank $[K:\mathbb Q]=2$; class groups and non-dyadic units contribute only finite groups. The invariant part under the quadratic involution has rank at least one, from the cyclotomic extension. If the inducing character is $\theta$, fixing the determinant fixes $\theta\theta^s$ on $G_K$. Thus at most one free character parameter remains. More explicitly, fixing the character on the image of $1+s$ fixes a sublattice of rank at least one; the finite index in its saturation contributes an integral, finite extension, not an extra parameter. The resulting complete character rings have integral dimension at most two. There are only finitely many possible residual characters, since their unordered pair is prescribed by $\bar r|_{G_K}$.

Their images in the trace space are closed of dimension at most two. Indeed, each generating character value satisfies $$X^2-T(h)X+\chi(h)=0\qquad(h\in G_K).$$ Finitely many generators of the abelian character group, followed by complete Nakayama, make each character ring finite over its trace image. This is the finite-character argument of (OpenAI 2026a, Proposition 3.1(3)) <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[FM2](#ref-FM2)].

For finite projective image, the classification of finite subgroups of $\operatorname{PGL}_2$ leaves cyclic and dihedral groups, already covered by reducibility and induction, and the three exceptional groups $A_4,S_4,A_5$. Their exponents divide $M=60$. For any matrix with projective order dividing $M$, the ratio $\zeta$ of its eigenvalues satisfies $\zeta^M=1$, and $$\frac{T(g)^2}{\chi(g)}=2+\zeta+\zeta^{-1}.$$ Consequently $T(g)$ is a root of the monic polynomial $$\prod_{\zeta^M=1}
